I don't keep up with the baking or the bonsai trees. Or the telescope. I used to have plans for my downtime, but now I just... don't do them.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're not good enough or blaming yourself for things more than usual?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I really couldn't guess. It's hard to say how I'd feel about a hypothetical. Maybe it would be fine. I just... I don't know.\n\nDoctor: Even if you haven’t been doing things like baking or using the telescope lately, can you remember if you started doing them again, would they still feel enjoyable or would they feel like a chore?\n\nPatient: I... I just don't have much energy left for anything else.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed feeling down or losing interest in things you usually enjoy, like hobbies or spending time with friends or family?\n\nPatient: I suppose so, yeah. It's mostly just... a low, gray mood, I guess. It just kind of runs in the background most days. \n\nI mean, I still go to the lab and talk to people just fine. I just... I don't really feel light or excited about things anymore. Like baking, or... taking out the telescope. I just don't really do those things the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about not feeling excited about things like baking or using the telescope? Do these activities feel like they don’t bring you any pleasure anymore, or do you just not feel like doing them?\n\nPatient: I guess... I just don't feel like doing them. It's not that I wouldn't like it if I did, I just... don't. \n\nI'm still getting my lab work done, mostly. But... things like grant drafts or just making routine decisions about projects... it takes me a lot longer than it used to. And at home, I just... I don't keep up with the baking or the bonsai trees. Or the telescope. I used to have plans for my downtime, but now I just... don't do them.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're not good enough or blaming yourself for things more than usual?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I mean... I guess I get frustrated when the house is a mess or when I'm slow at work, but... I wouldn't say I'm blaming myself more than usual. It's just how it is.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're not worth much or that you're failing others, even when there's no clear reason for it?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't feel like I'm failing anyone. I just... I just don't have a lot of energy for things. That's all.\n\nDoctor: Has there been a time recently when you noticed you didn’t care about things that usually matter to you, like your hobbies or even work?\n\nPatient: I don't think it's that I don't care. I mean... I still care about my research and everything. I just... like I said before, I don't really have the energy to actually do the extra stuff right now. It's not about not caring.\n\nDoctor: When you say you don’t do things like baking or using the telescope anymore, is it because you don’t enjoy them as much as you used to, or is it more about not feeling like doing them even if you might still enjoy them?\n\nPatient: I guess... it's just the energy part. Like, I think I'd still like them if I actually did them. I just... I don't feel like dragging the telescope out or getting all the ingredients together. It's just too much effort right now.\n\nDoctor: When you think about things you used to really enjoy, like baking or using the telescope, do you still feel any pleasure or satisfaction when you do them now?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I haven't... I haven't actually done them lately to find out. So it's hard to say.\n\nDoctor: When you think about doing things you used to really enjoy, like baking or using the telescope, does the thought of doing them feel as pleasant to you now as it used to?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I guess... it's hard to imagine right now. I just haven't really thought about it.\n\nDoctor: When you go through your day, do you find that nothing really lifts your mood, even when something good happens?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, if something good happens, I... I guess I still feel okay about it. It's not like I don't feel anything at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that when you do take the time to bake or look through the telescope, you don't feel the same enjoyment or satisfaction from them as you used to?\n\nPatient: Um... like I said, I just haven't actually tried doing them lately. So... I really couldn't say.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that even when you do manage to start doing something you used to enjoy, like baking or looking through the telescope, it doesn’t feel as satisfying or enjoyable as it used to?\n\nPatient: Um... didn't you just ask me that? Like I said... I haven't actually managed to start doing them lately. So I really don't know how it would feel.\n\nDoctor: Even if you haven't done things like baking or using the telescope lately, do you think you'd feel the same joy or pleasure if you did them now, or do you think they wouldn't feel as good as they used to?\n\nPatient: I... I really couldn't guess. It's hard to say how I'd feel about a hypothetical. Maybe it would be fine. I just... I don't know.\n\nDoctor: Even if you haven’t been doing things like baking or using the telescope lately, can you remember if you started doing them again, would they still feel enjoyable or would they feel like a chore?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know how else to answer this. You keep asking me the same thing. I really just don't know.\n\nDoctor: Can you think back to the last time you did something you used to really enjoy, like baking or using the telescope — did it feel as pleasurable or satisfying as it used to, or did it feel like it wasn't as enjoyable as before?\n\nPatient: I... I think we're just going in circles here. Like I said, I haven't done them in a long time. I don't remember the last time. So I can't tell you how it felt.\n\nDoctor: Can you think back to when you were regularly doing things like baking or using the telescope — did you ever notice that even when you did them, they didn’t feel as enjoyable or rewarding as they used to?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. When I was actually doing them, they were fine. I just... stopped. I really don't have a different answer for you about this.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall if you ever stopped feeling pleasure or enjoyment in activities you used to really like, such as baking or using the telescope, while you were still doing them regularly?\n\nPatient: I... I really don't know what else to say. I've already told you, when I did them, they were fine. I just don't have anything else to add to that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
